🎧 Audio Brief ↗
Wed Sep 30 · 2:00 PM · Campbell 2nd DEXA · conversion moment M · 77 · 5'7"
Time unconfirmed: 2 PM is the old Sep 23 slot, same as Robert 4 Week ended Sep 23: no current package 11 of 12 sessions through Sep 22 Left hip tweak Sep 13, never followed up Header date and audio brief are from Sep 23
🎯 Goals & Motivation
  • Lean mass gain, BUILD ONLY. Read off his ALMI (4th percentile, well under the 60th), not an agreement. It matches what he says he wants; let him own it out loud.
  • Why: longevity and independence, "I like to keep moving," into his nineties. He turned the low ALMI at the intro into a target himself; muscle is insurance against decline. Why band: specific and anchored.
  • Focus: 3x/week lifting (up from 2) near failure, 120 g protein, carb timing around tennis-then-weights days (added Sep 8), visceral fat through food quality, not a deficit.
  • The why has no scene in it. "Capable at 90" is a state, not a picture. His 6-to-12-month answer is the most valuable thing to leave the room with.
🏆 Wins to Open With Scan 2 Pending
  • 11 of 12 sessions (91.7%) through Sep 22, three lifting days on top of tennis Mon, Tue and Thu, at 77. The one miss, Sun Sep 6, he called himself after back soreness and a low deep-sleep reading. Name it as good judgment.
  • Lat pulldown beat its target: 67.5 for 12, 12, 12 on Sep 15, then 72.5 on Sep 17 (the ask), then 75 for 12, 11, 8 on Sep 22.
  • Carbs in the day after the Sep 8 call. Sep 9 journal: "Had a banana and shake before weight training and a shake and a cracker after."
  • Logged exactly what was asked. 40 journal entries Aug 28 to Sep 10; asked Sep 8 for "just two more days," he did Sep 9 and 10 and stopped. Compliance, not a drop-off.

She peaked at a hundred and six point eight pounds of lean in June and she is now at a hundred and one point five, so five and a third pounds gone in three months, and her ALMI has slid from the sixty seventh percentile to the fifty ninth. That is the cost of sixty nine days without a session. Body fat is forty four point seven, the eighty ninth percentile for her age, but naming that as the problem today would be true and useless. Visceral is two twenty five, still down five on her February baseline, though climbing three scans running.
4. The context you cannot skip. Her eyes. A second ophthalmologist diagnosed hyperkeratosis and cornea changes from contact lens overuse on the seventeenth of September, and she cannot wear contacts at all. She is a dentist working without the magnification she relies on. Her words were, it went from nothing's wrong to I can't tolerate anything within two months. The gym has been paused on your instruction, her program is walks only, and nothing at all is assigned between now and the scan. Do not read the zero percent as non compliance. She does not tick boxes, she never has, and her step count says she is moving more than she has all year.
5. How to run the room. She answers honestly and she names her own misses before you can, so do not catch her, just give her one thing. Check how the flight went before you read the lean number, because jet lag and dehydration move it. Two days of food photos should be waiting from Friday and Saturday. The next block is a restart, not a resume, she never did a single session of the phase two that landed on the twenty fourth of August, so build it around her eyes and ask whether overhead pressing provokes anything before you program it. No calorie target, no bone density, no gold standards, and do not chase her eye results.

This is the single biggest lever on the board. Sets two and three at 18 reps on a 15-rep target is not a hard session, it is a light one. She dropped leg press from 130 on Sep 21 to 110 on Sep 26 and then out-repped the target by three. Sep 7 already covered the fix: two genuinely hard sets beat three moderate ones. Pyramiding up is explicitly wrong here. Suggest the variable, load, and let her report the number back.

🎯 Scan-Day Focus
  1. Frame the window before any number. 27 days, Sep 7 to Oct 4, three trained weeks and a six-day retreat inside it. Say out loud that fat versus lean is the read.

  4. Name the “did I lose my gains” fear before she raises it. She said it on Sep 24 and then dropped leg press by 20. Connect it: the weights felt heavy, so she went lighter, and going lighter is what keeps the lean tissue from coming back.
  5. Make the intensity fix concrete, not conceptual. Two hard sets beat three moderate ones, and she has heard the principle twice. Show her Sep 26 on the screen: 110 for 18 and 18. One number to change per lift.
  6. Protein floor, not protein average. The 74 and the 112 are the gap, and both were days without a real-food protein meal. Three fist-sized protein meals plus two shakes, Greek yogurt at night if needed. No gram totals asked of her.

  • Dumbbell bench is going backwards while everything else climbs. Epley est 1RM 70 → 60 → 57 across Sep 11, 18 and 25, at a fixed 45. Her injury record says the left-quad spasm activates during bench, so ask before prescribing: is this the quad or is this strength? The answer changes what we do.
  • The RDL has a real ceiling and it is equipment, not effort. Parked at 70 since Sep 14 and she raised it herself on Sep 17: the Bowflex dumbbells are clunky, there are no safeties, and racking them after a heavy set frightens her. She is right to be careful with an L3 to L5 history. Solve the setup, do not push the load. The kickstand deadlift went 45 to 50 this week, so there is a hinge she is happy to load.
  • Food boredom is the adherence risk. No Cow, Owen bars, smoothies and vegan burgers on rotation, and she flagged it herself. Protein is short at about 116 g against a 140 target, and variety is the lever that moves it.
  • No food logging since Sep 22, seven days. Macros Journal total 0, photo journal silent. The 2-day task is live on Oct 3 and Oct 4 and Send 3 points at it.
  • Sep 23 session still carries 1 unread member comment. That session contains the RDL and the outstanding ask was a deadlift video. Neither the API nor UI clicks would open it this run. Read it before asking her for the video again, and nothing in this week’s drafts asks.
  • She takes a target the first time it is given. Asked on Sep 22 for Single Arm Row at 30, she trained it at 30 on Sep 28 and put three other lifts up in the same session. That is the single best argument for giving her more specific numbers, not fewer.
  • Nothing is mechanically owed to her. The last message in the thread is hers, “Thanks!” Sep 22 5:33 PM, a thank-you rather than a question. His question about the quad was left unanswered by her, not by us.
  • Effort is not the problem. 12 of 13 sessions, four rated “Difficult”, intensifiers being used, loads climbing on six lifts. If lean fell again it is a fueling decision we got wrong, not effort she withheld, and it should be owned that way.
  • Quad activation: not coach debt, but the loose end is now a week old. Re-verified absent from all six upcoming sessions including P3 (0 hits for activation, foam, couch or massage). Daniel ruled on 22 Sep that this was always hers to do and never a program change, and the live Sep 17 session note files it under Mobility as hers. But the 22 Sep DM told her “I will get a quad activation piece built into the front of your sessions”, so either build it into P3 or tell her plainly that the foam roller work is the whole of it. Nothing in this week’s drafts mentions it in any tense.
  • VAT is 88.0, above the 80 line, but falling at every scan (113.0 → 109.9 → 91.6 → 88.0) and the visceral-strategy box is unticked with her 10-day moment long past (Jun 10). Not a send and not a recommendation, just a number he may want to name: he told her on Sep 3 that under 80 was the next target and she is 8 away.

🎯 Scan-Day Focus
  1. Frame the window before any number. 32 days, Sep 3 to Oct 5, and say plainly that the read is fat versus lean, not the scale.
  2. Open on the four months, not the four weeks. 21.5 lb down since May, 12.9 lb of it fat, body fat percentile 40th to 21st, visceral fat 113 to 88 and falling at every scan. That is a genuine transformation and it is hers.
  3. Then go straight at lean, and own it. 8.51 lb of lean gone since May and ALMI 59th to 31st. She trained 12 of 13 and rated four of them Difficult, so this is a fueling call we got wrong on a GLP-1, not effort she withheld.
  4. Say the thing about goal weight. She is 2 lb from the number she came in wanting. From here, more weight off is lean off. The job changes today from losing to holding, and she needs to hear that explicitly or she will keep chasing the scale.
  5. Protein is the lever, and name the gap. Her last complete day was about 116 g against 140. That is roughly one more solid feeding, not a diet change. Leucine or the EAAs cover the meals that land small.
  6. Fix the food boredom rather than repeating the number. She raised it herself. Two or three new items off the recipe list will move protein further than another reminder.
  7. Credit the Single Arm Row before anything else in the training conversation. She was given 30 and took 30 within a week, and put three more lifts up the same session. Name it.
  8. Ask about the bench before prescribing. Epley est 1RM 70 to 60 to 57 at a fixed 45. Her notes say the left quad fires during bench. Find out which it is, then decide.
  9. Ask his own unanswered question: how has the quad behaved over the last few workouts?
  10. Solve the deadlift setup, do not push the load. She is at her comfort ceiling on the Bowflex dumbbells with no safeties and an L3 to L5 history. Kickstand deadlift (already at 50), a trap-bar style hold, lighter with a slower eccentric, or a different hinge. Suggest, do not mandate.
  11. Close the quad activation loop one way or the other. She was told in writing on 22 Sep that it would be built into the front of her sessions. Either build it into P3 or tell her the foam roller work is the whole of it.
  12. Read the Sep 23 comment before the room, in case the deadlift video is already sitting in it.
  13. Ask the “what have you noticed” question live if she has not replied. Build prompts, not fat-loss prompts: shoulders and sleeves, fuller not smaller, weights lighter in the hand, how the back is holding up.
  14. Leave out: bone density (32 days is noise), any calorie target, the ALMI kg/m² value, and the bench, squat and deadlift Gold Standards.
  15. No renewal question, no close, no Slack post. Routine package checkpoint inside a term running to Dec 29, 2026.

Gold Standards — Female, 60+ · 215.0 lb (Scan 1 bodyweight)
BB Bench—~160 lb 1RM (0.75x BW)
BB Squat—~270 lb 1RM (1.25x BW)
BB Deadlift—~320 lb 1RM (1.5x BW)
Do NOT put the Gold Standards in front of her on Monday. Two reasons, and both of them bite here. The multipliers key off bodyweight, so at 215 lb hers are inflated and they will fall as she leans out, which is the opposite of the signal we want for a fat-loss member. And she is four weeks into her first structured program, at 61, training at home on adjustable dumbbells with a sensitive lower back and no barbell anywhere in the plan. A 320 lb deadlift target reads as impossible, not aspirational. Use direction instead: her own numbers in four weeks (goblet squat 15 to 20, Romanian deadlift 20 to 30, bent over row 20 to 30, all inside eight days), and holding ALMI at the 80th percentile while fat mass comes down. Those are the benchmarks that apply to her.

🎯 Scan-Day Focus
  1. Frame the window first. 26 days, Sep 9 to Oct 5, and nearly two weeks of it abroad. Say plainly that the read is lean versus fat, not the scale.
  2. Open on visceral fat and on her steps. 126.2 to 114.2 cm², and 15,170 steps a day averaged across the last week while travelling. Both are hers and neither shows up on her bathroom scale, which is the thing she said was not showing her anything.
  3. Then say the thing about 128 lb. She is 137.2 with lean already down a pound. From here, more weight off is lean off. The job changes today from losing to building, and she needs to hear it in those words.
  4. Go at the ALMI number and own the trade. 23rd to 20th percentile, and every ounce of the lean came off the arms. Part of that is a rest we chose for the elbow. Name it as a decision, then say what the next block does about it. Her long-term marker is the 70th percentile, set at intro, and she remembers it.
  5. Credit the Arnold press before criticising the rest. Sep 29: 35 for 9, then 26 for 10. That is the pattern he has asked for three times, done unprompted, in a hotel gym. Then ask why the row is still 25 for 10, 10, 10 four sessions running.
  6. Carbs, not protein. Protein has been said three times. Carbs around training have not, and at 104 to 135 g against this activity level they are the reason hard sets feel harder than they should. This is also the natural moment for the pre/post workout nutrition strategy, which has never been sent. No calorie number.
  7. Settle the equipment question out loud. She is home with a full gym, and the block he has built for the next five weeks is dumbbells only. If that is deliberate, tell her why. If it is not, this is the moment to change it.
  8. Ask about the elbow before prescribing anything upper body. It has not improved across two calls, it has cost her 20 on the bent over row, and the form assessment is not until Oct 22.
  9. Ask about the knees and the hack squat angle. One question, then decide. Suggest a variable, do not drop a movement.
  10. Close the referral question she asked on 16 Sep.
  11. Ask the “what have you noticed” question live if she has not replied. Recomp prompts: looser in some places and tighter in others, the scale barely moving while clothes change, how her legs and back held up at 15,000 steps a day abroad.
  12. Leave out: bone density (26 days is noise), any calorie target, the ALMI kg/m² value, the bench, squat and deadlift Gold Standards, and any phase-transition announcement.
  13. No renewal question, no close, no Slack post. Routine package checkpoint inside a term running to Feb 3, 2027.
